                                    I.   INTRODUCTION

A.     Purpose

This study addresses the traffic impacts associated with the proposed Beehive Academy Charter
School development located in Draper, Utah. The proposed project is located at approximately
325 East 12200 South. Figure 1 shows a vicinity map of the proposed development.

Included within the analyses for this study are the traffic operations and recommended mitigation
measures for existing conditions and plus project conditions (conditions after development of the
proposed project) at key intersections and roadways near the site. Future 2025 conditions were
also analyzed.




         Figure 1: Vicinity map showing the project location in Draper, Utah




                                                                                               1
                                                              Beehive Academy Charter School
                                                                                         Traffic Impact Study



B.     Scope

The study area was defined based on conversations with the development team. This study was
scoped to evaluate the traffic operational performance impacts of the project on the following
intersections:
        • 12200 South / 300 East
        • Retail Access / 300 East
        • 300 East / 12300 South (SR-71)
        • 450 East / 12300 South (SR-71)
        • 12230 South / 450 East
        • 12200 South / 700 East (SR-71)
        • Project Accesses (2) / 12200 South
        • Project Access / 300 East

C.     Analysis Methodology

Level of service (LOS) is a term that describes the operating performance of an intersection or
roadway. LOS is measured quantitatively and reported on a scale from A to F, with A representing
the best performance and F the worst. Table 1 provides a brief description of each LOS letter
designation and an accompanying average delay per vehicle for both signalized and unsignalized
intersections. Figure 2 provides a visual representation of each LOS letter designation.

The Highway Capacity Manual (HCM), 6th Edition, 2016 methodology was used in this study to
remain consistent with “state-of-the-practice” professional standards. This methodology has
different quantitative evaluations for signalized and unsignalized intersections. For signalized and
all-way stop intersections, the LOS is provided for the overall intersection (weighted average of
all approach delays). For all other unsignalized intersections, LOS is reported based on the worst
approach.

Using Synchro/SimTraffic software, which follow the HCM methodology, the peak hour LOS was
computed for each study intersection. Multiple runs of SimTraffic were used to provide a statistical
evaluation of the interaction between the intersections. The detailed LOS reports are provided in
Appendix B. Hales Engineering also calculated the 95th percentile queue lengths for the study
intersections using SimTraffic. The detailed queue length reports are provided in Appendix D.

D.     Level of Service Standards

For the purposes of this study, a minimum acceptable intersection performance for each of the
study intersections was set at LOS D. If levels of service E or F conditions exist, an explanation
and/or mitigation measures will be presented. A LOS D threshold is consistent with “state-of-the-
practice” traffic engineering principles for urbanized areas.




                                                                                                  2
                                                               Beehive Academy Charter School
                                                                                               Traffic Impact Study

               II.   EXISTING (2020) BACKGROUND CONDITIONS

A.     Purpose

The purpose of the background analysis is to study the intersections and roadways during the
peak travel periods of the day with background traffic and geometric conditions. Through this
analysis, background traffic operational deficiencies can be identified, and potential mitigation
measures recommended. This analysis provides a baseline condition that may be compared to
the build conditions to identify the impacts of the development.

B.     Roadway System

The primary roadways that will provide access to the project site are described below:

12200 South – is a city-maintained roadway which is classified by the Draper City Master
Transportation Plan (MTP) (November 2019) as a “commercial minor collector.” According to the
MTP, the typical cross section for this type of roadway is one travel lane in each direction with a
center two-way left-turn lane (TWLTL). There is currently a gap in this roadway between 300 East
and approximately 400 East. According to the MTP, improvements to 12200 South are planned
between 300 East and 700 East as a Phase 2 (2025-2034) project. These improvements will
include construction of the unbuilt portion of the roadway between 300 East and 400 East and
widening of the remainder of the segment. The posted speed limit is 25 mph in the study area.

300 East – is a city-maintained roadway which is classified by the Draper City Master
Transportation Plan (MTP) (November 2019) as a “minor collector.” 300 East currently exists with
one travel lane in each direction and a two-way left-turn lane (TWLTL). The posted speed limit is
25 mph in the study area.

C.     Traffic Volumes

Weekday morning (7:00 to 9:00 a.m.) and afternoon (2:00 to 4:00 p.m.) peak period traffic counts
were performed at the following intersections:
      • 12200 South / 300 East
      • Retail Access / 300 East
      • 300 East / 12300 South (SR-71)
      • 450 East / 12300 South (SR-71)
      • 12230 South / 450 East
      • 12200 South / 700 East (SR-71)

The counts were performed on Wednesday, September 11, 2019. (Additional data was collected
on Thursday, September 19, 2019.) The morning peak hour was determined to be between 7:45
and 8:45 a.m., and the afternoon peak hour was determined to be between 3:00 and 4:00 p.m.


                                                                                                 5
                                                                                               Beehive Academy Charter School
                                                                                                                                         Traffic Impact Study


The morning and afternoon peak hour volumes were relatively equal. Because school land uses
tend to have greater impacts on the surrounding roadways during the morning peak hour, the
morning peak hour was used in the analysis to represent the worst-case conditions. Detailed
count data are included in Appendix A.

The counts collected in 2019 were increased to represent existing (2020) conditions. Based on
historical traffic data, traffic on 12300 South increases by approximately 3% per year. Therefore,
the 2019 counts were increased by approximately 3% to estimate 2020 conditions. Figure 3
shows the existing morning peak hour volumes as well as intersection geometry at the study
intersections.

D.        Level of Service Analysis

Hales Engineering determined that the 300 East / 12300 South and 450 East / 12300 South
intersections operate at poor levels of service in existing (2020) background conditions. All other
study intersections are currently operating at acceptable levels of service during the morning peak
hour, as shown in Table 2.

E.        Queuing Analysis

Hales Engineering calculated the 95th percentile queue lengths for each of the study intersections.
95th percentile queue lengths of approximately 600 feet and 1,000 feet are anticipated on the
eastbound and northbound approaches of the 300 East / 12300 South intersection during the
morning peak hour.

F.        Mitigation Measures

The current traffic volumes at the 300 East / 12300 South intersection warrant dual left-turn lanes
on the northbound approach, based on UDOT left-turn guidelines. It is recommended that these
northbound dual left-turn lanes be installed when possible to reduce delays and queueing.

The poor LOS at the 450 East / 12300 South is due to high volumes on 12300 South that leave
few gaps for vehicles to turn left from 450 East. However, this delay represents less than 10
vehicles turning left onto 12300 South. Vehicles will learn to re-route to traffic signals to avoid
delays as needed. The 450 East / 12300 South intersection was analyzed in 2017 for a signal
warrant by UDOT. It was found that a signal is warranted. However, the signal was put on hold
and has currently not been installed. Hales Engineering recommends that this signal be
constructed as soon as possible to provide an adequate level of service at this location.

G.        Mitigated Scenario

Hales Engineering completed a mitigated scenario assuming northbound dual left-turn lanes at
the 300 East / 12300 South intersection, as warranted. It was also assumed that the 450 East /
12300 South signal has been built as a mitigation. As shown in Table 3, it is anticipated that all
study intersections will operate at acceptable levels of service during the morning peak hour with
the proposed mitigations except for the 450 East / 12300 South intersection as previously
discussed. These results serve as a baseline condition for the impact analysis of the proposed
development during existing (2020) conditions.

                               III. PROJECT CONDITIONS

A.     Purpose

The project conditions discussion explains the type and intensity of development. This provides
the basis for trip generation, distribution, and assignment of project trips to the surrounding study
intersections defined in Chapter I.

B.     Project Description

This study addresses the traffic impacts associated with the proposed Beehive Academy Charter
School development located in Draper, Utah. The school will be for grades K-12 and will be
located at approximately 325 East 12200 South. A concept plan for the proposed development is
provided in Appendix C.

The proposed land use for the development has been identified as follows:
       • Charter School (K-12)                                  1,000 Students

C.     Trip Generation

Hales Engineering has collected peak hour trip generation data at several charter schools along
the Wasatch Front. Hales Engineering has observed peak hour trip generation rates ranging from
0.3 trips per student to 0.83 AM trips per student, 0.32 to 0.72 PM trips per student, with an
average of 0.65 AM trips and 0.57 PM trips per student.

With the proposed project being a K-12 school, Hales Engineering referred to local K-12 charter
school data for the analysis. The nearby American Preparatory Academy (APA) charter school in
Draper was observed to have a rate of 0.80 trips per student during the morning peak hour and
0.60 trips per student during the afternoon peak hour. These rates are similar to the rates for ITE’s
“Private School (K-12)” land use. Trip generation for the proposed project is included in Table 4.

The total trip generation for the development is as follows:
       • Morning Peak Hour:                                          800 trips
       • Afternoon Peak Hour:                                        600 trips




                                                                                                   9
                                                              Beehive Academy Charter School
                                                                                         Traffic Impact Study



                                   Table 4: Trip Generation




D.     Trip Distribution and Assignment

Project traffic is assigned to the roadway network based on the type of trip and the proximity of
project access points to major streets, high population densities, and regional trip attractions.
Existing travel patterns observed during data collection also provide helpful guidance to
establishing these distribution percentages, especially near the site. The resulting distribution of
project generated trips during the morning peak hour is as follows:

To/From Project:
      • 20% North
      • 5% South
      • 50% West
      • 25% East

These trip distribution assumptions were used to assign the morning peak hour generated traffic
at the study intersections to create trip assignment for the proposed development. Based on
discussions with the development team, incoming trips will be routed through the 450 East
intersections to create a one-way circulation for inbound traffic, with outbound traffic using the
300 East intersections. Trips were assigned to enter the development site through a westbound
right-turn movement into the Access 1 / 12200 South intersection. Trip assignment for the
development is shown in Figure 4.

E.     Access

The proposed access for the site will be gained at the following locations (see also concept plan
in Appendix C):

Proposed New Road (12200 / 12230 South):
      • A new road has been proposed to connect 12230 South to 12200 South and extend
         to connect with the 300 East roundabout. The two primary accesses to the school are
         planned to connect to this new roadway.
      • Access 1 will be located 600 feet east of the 12200 South / 300 East roundabout, on
         the southeast corner of the property. It is anticipated that this access will be ingress
         only to facilitate traffic flow during drop-off and pick-up times at the school. Based on
         discussions with the development team, parents will be advised to enter this access
         using the westbound right-turn movement and connecting from 12300 South via 450
         East. It is anticipated that the access will be stop-controlled.
      • Access 2 will be located approximately 400 feet east of the 12200 South / 300 East
         roundabout. It is anticipated that this access will be egress only and stop-controlled at
         the access.

300 East:
      • Access 3 will be located approximately 350 feet north of the 12200 South / 300 East
          roundabout, on the northwest corner of the property. It is anticipated to be a limited
          access, primarily serving for school egress during the afternoon pick-up period. It is
          anticipated that this access will be closed during the morning drop-off time.




                                                                                               12
                                                               Beehive Academy Charter School
                                                                                          Traffic Impact Study



              IV. EXISTING (2020) PLUS PROJECT CONDITIONS

A.     Purpose

The purpose of the existing (2020) plus project analysis is to study the intersections and roadways
during the peak travel periods of the day for existing background traffic and geometric conditions
plus the net trips generated by the proposed development. This scenario provides valuable insight
into the potential impacts of the proposed project on background traffic conditions.

B.     Traffic Volumes

Hales Engineering added the project trips discussed in Chapter III to the existing (2020)
background traffic volumes to predict turning movement volumes for existing (2020) plus project
conditions. Existing (2020) plus project morning peak hour turning movement volumes are shown
in Figure 5.

C.     Level of Service Analysis

Hales Engineering determined that the signalized 300 East / 12300 South will operate at a LOS
E with project traffic added. Additionally, the Retail Access / 300 East is anticipated to operate at
LOS F during the morning peak hour and the 12200 South / 700 East intersection is anticipated
to operate at LOS E. All other intersections are anticipated to operate at acceptable levels of
service during the morning peak hour with project traffic added, as shown in Table 5.

D.     Queuing Analysis

Hales Engineering calculated the 95th percentile queue lengths for each of the study intersections.
Significant queuing of up to 950 feet is anticipated on the eastbound approach of the 300 East /
12300 South intersection due to insufficient left-turn turning lane length. This queuing is likely to
interfere with operations at the neighboring 210 East / 12300 South signal. No other significant
queuing is anticipated during the morning peak hour.

E.     Mitigation Measures

Hales Engineering recommends that the eastbound left-turn lane storage be extended at the 300
East / 12300 South intersection. It is also recommended that southbound right-turn storage be
increased to better accommodate heavy volumes on that movement.

It was determined that left-turn traffic from the Retail Access / 300 East intersection will likely
reroute to avoid delays during the morning peak hour. This may entail making a right-turn
movement onto 12300 South at a different access or making a right-turn onto 300 East and a U-
turn movement at the 12200 South roundabout.



                                                                                                  13
                                                                                               Beehive Academy Charter School
                                                                                                                                         Traffic Impact Study


Hales Engineering recommends that an eastbound right-turn lane be added at the 12200 South /
700 East intersection to allow for less delay and to mitigate the anticipated poor level of service.
The Draper City Transportation Master Plan (November 2019) includes plans to widen 700 East
to a 5-lane cross-section by the year 2025. It is anticipated that this improvement will also
decrease delay on the side street in future years.

                V. FUTURE (2025) BACKGROUND CONDITIONS

A.     Purpose

The purpose of the future (2025) background analysis is to study the intersections and roadways
during the peak travel periods of the day for future background traffic and geometric conditions.
Through this analysis, future background traffic operational deficiencies can be identified, and
potential mitigation measures recommended.

B.     Roadway Network

The Draper City Transportation Master Plan (November 2019) indicates plans to widen 700 East
to a 5-lane cross-section by the year 2025. It was assumed that this widening project had taken
place for future (2025) background conditions. According to the Wasatch Front Regional Council
(WFRC) Regional Transportation Plan, there are no additional projects planned before 2025 in
the study area. Therefore, no additional changes were made to the roadway network aside from
the planned 700 East widening for the future (2025) analysis.

C.     Traffic Volumes

Hales Engineering estimated future (2025) forecasted volumes using existing local ADT growth
rate information published by UDOT. A growth rate of approximately 1% per year was applied to
the current traffic volumes in the study area. Future (2025) morning peak hour turning movement
volumes are shown in Figure 6.

D.     Level of Service Analysis

Hales Engineering determined that the 300 East / 12300 South intersection is anticipated to
operate at LOS E during the morning peak hour in future (2025) background traffic conditions. All
other study intersections are anticipated to operate at acceptable levels of service during the
morning peak hour in future (2025) background conditions, as shown in Table 7.

E.     Queuing Analysis

Hales Engineering calculated the 95th percentile queue lengths for each of the study intersections.
Excessive queuing of up to approximately 775 feet was observed on the eastbound approach of
the 300 East / 12300 South intersection. No other significant queuing is anticipated during the
morning peak hour.

F.       Mitigation Measures

The Draper City Transportation Master Plan (November 2019) indicates a phase 2 (2025-2034)
plan to widen 12300 South to a 7-lane cross-section from I-15 to 700 East. Hales Engineering
recommends that UDOT proceeds forward with this plan as background conditions show that this
is needed by the year 2025. No other mitigation measures are recommended for future (2025)
background conditions.

hour conditions with 12300 south widened to a 7-lane cross-section in the project area. LOS
results for this mitigated scenario are shown in Table 8. These results serve as a baseline
condition for the impact analysis of the proposed development for future (2025) conditions.

               VI. FUTURE (2025) PLUS PROJECT CONDITIONS

A.     Purpose

The purpose of the future (2025) plus project analysis is to study the intersections and roadways
during the peak travel periods of the day for future background traffic and geometric conditions
plus the net trips generated by the proposed development. This scenario provides valuable insight
into the potential impacts of the proposed project on future background traffic conditions.

B.     Traffic Volumes

Hales Engineering added the project trips discussed in Chapter III to the future (2025) background
traffic volumes to predict turning movement volumes for future (2025) plus project conditions.
Future (2025) plus project morning peak hour turning movement volumes are shown in Figure 7.

C.     Level of Service Analysis

Hales Engineering determined that all study intersections are anticipated to operate at acceptable
levels of service during the morning peak hour in future (2025) plus project conditions, as shown
in Table 9.

D.     Queuing Analysis

Hales Engineering calculated the 95th percentile queue lengths for each of the study intersections.
No significant queuing is anticipated during the morning peak hour.

E.     Mitigation Measures

No mitigation measures are recommended.

             VII. AFTERNOON PEAK HOUR CONDITIONS (2025)

A.     Purpose

The purpose of the future (2025) plus project analysis is to study the intersections and roadways
during the peak travel periods of the day for future background traffic and geometric conditions
as well as the net trips generated by the proposed development. These scenarios provide
valuable insight into the potential impacts of the proposed project on future background traffic
conditions.

B.     Traffic Volumes

Hales Engineering estimated future (2025) forecasted volumes using existing local ADT growth
rate information published by UDOT. A growth rate of approximately 1% per year was applied to
the current traffic volumes in the study area. Future (2025) afternoon peak hour turning movement
volumes are shown in Figure 8.

An afternoon peak hour trip assignment was created utilizing the same trip distribution
assumptions and afternoon peak hour generated trips as previously discussed in Chapter III of
this report. The afternoon peak hour trip assignment is shown in Figure 9.

Hales Engineering added the project trips discussed in Chapter III to the future (2025) background
traffic volumes to predict turning movement volumes for future (2025) plus project conditions.
Future (2025) plus project afternoon peak hour turning movement volumes are shown in Figure
10.

C.     Level of Service Analysis

Hales Engineering determined that all study intersections, excluding the previously mentioned
retail access, are anticipated to operate at acceptable levels of service during the afternoon peak
hour in future (2025) background conditions, as shown in Table 10.

However, it was determined that the 300 East / 12300 South and the Access 3 / 300 East
intersections would fail during afternoon peak hour conditions in future (2025) conditions with
project traffic added, as shown in Table 11. The excessive queuing of more than 800 feet in the
southbound direction on 300 East negatively impacts traffic upstream at the 12200 South
roundabout and at the northwest project access (Access 3).

D.        Queuing Analysis

Hales Engineering calculated the 95th percentile queue lengths for each of the study intersections.
Queuing of more than 800 feet was observed on the southbound approach at the 300 East /
12300 South intersection, backing past the retail access and the 12200 South roundabout. This
excessive queuing is anticipated to negatively affect the project access onto 300 East, causing a
failing level of service at that access.

E.        Mitigation Measures

Hales Engineering recommends that southbound dual left-turn lanes be installed at the 300 East
/ 12300 South intersection. It is anticipated that these left-turn lanes will be warranted (UDOT)
with project traffic added during the afternoon peak hour in future (2025) conditions. No other
mitigation measures are recommended for this scenario.

F.        Mitigated Scenario

project conditions with the mentioned mitigation measures applied to the roadway network. The
LOS results of this analysis are shown in Table 12.

G.     On-site Circulation and Queuing Analysis

The on-site circulation and queuing analysis were completed in VISSIM to closely represent the
on-site out-loading conditions. It was assumed that the student pick-up areas at the school were
approximately 325 feet on the east pick-up area and 300 feet on the north pick-up area for a total
of 625 feet of pick-up area on the school property. It is anticipated that the north pick-up area will
be for high-school age students while the east pick-up area will be for lower grade levels. Multiple
runs were used to provide a statistical evaluation of the site circulation operations. The 95th
percentile maximum queue length was found to be approximately 2,250 linear feet during the
afternoon peak hour. This assumed an enrollment of 1,000 students and that all students are
dismissed at the same time. It is recommended that the school site be designed to accommodate
this queue length onsite, or measures be taken to reduce the required queue length. Such
measures include shuttles, a carpool program, or staggered dismissal times.

Based on this analysis, Hales Engineering recommends that a total queue storage length of at
least 2,250 feet (not including the actual pick-up area) be implemented into the property to ensure
proper circulation and avoid backing into the roadways adjacent to the property. Existing plans
indicate five stacking lanes, each approximately 500 feet long with an additional 100 feet of length
between the pick-up areas. This allows for approximately 2,600 feet of queuing length outside of
the pick-up zones and is deemed satisfactory for the existing charter school site. It is also
recommended that traffic between the north pick-up zones and east pick-up zones be balanced
as equally as possible to avoid any potential complications from traffic in one pick-up area being
heavier than the other.

H.     Recommended Storage Lengths

Hales Engineering determined recommended storage lengths based on the 95th percentile queue
lengths given in the future (2025) plus project scenario. These storage lengths do not include the
taper length. Recommended storage lengths for the study intersections are shown in Table 13.
Intersections shown in Table 13 include new intersections and existing intersections that have
recommended storage length changes.

                        Table 13: Recommended Storage Lengths
